# FY2024 rendered-account breakdown audit

Updated August 29, 2026.

> Baseline note: the complete inventory and its 530/1291 counts below describe the pre-remediation browser snapshot. The verified follow-up changes immediately below are current; the long baseline inventory is retained so no original violation silently disappears from the research trail.

## Outcome

The active data now meets the account-level invariant without inventing allocations. All **85 federal Treasury account roots** at or above $10 billion have an exact nested source schedule; **zero** remain standalone. No rendered breakdown contains only one nonzero row. The sole one-account MTS wrapper is retained only because its account is itself expanded into an exact statutory-source schedule.

The 50 states pass the stricter $5 billion browser-visible check: **483** large rows comprise **141** exact additive breakdowns and **342** explicitly labeled public-source disclosure ceilings. A ceiling is not claimed as a breakdown: it identifies the lowest official same-basis row currently published, and is retained instead of fabricating subaccounts.

The 530 large-row and 1,291 single-child counts in the inventory below are the pre-remediation baseline, not the current browser state. They are retained as an evidence trail.

This file is research evidence only. It does not change production data or relax accounting-basis, privacy, or source-precision controls.

### Continued federal account remediation

USAspending's official account-data model is now used as a lower-level research source for Treasury availability rows. Agencies submit cumulative financial data by Treasury Account Symbol (TAS), and USAspending's annual view uses the latest submission for the fiscal year. The implementation accepts a panel only when published child rows plus any explicitly labeled signed cross-source bridge reproduce the Treasury parent in integer cents; bridge amounts are never allocated to programs. [USAspending data reformatting documentation](https://github.com/fedspendingtransparency/usaspending-api/blob/master/data_reformatting.md) · [USAspending API endpoints](https://api.usaspending.gov/docs/endpoints)

- PBGC TAS `016-2021/2030-4204-000` reconciles to Treasury without a bridge: four USAspending program-activity/object-class rows total $14.65806273578B, and the $14.63848532416B Special Financial Assistance row is expanded into 27 exact PBGC plan payments.
- CBP, FEMA Disaster Relief, HUD Tenant-Based Rental Assistance, and VA Medical Community Care now have exact nested availability-row schedules. Every terminal child is below $10B. FEMA and HUD reuse their existing official declaration- and state-level schedules through visible signed basis bridges; no difference is assigned to a disaster, state, or program.
- Eight Navy, Army, Air Force, and Defense-Wide O&M availability rows for 2024 and 2023 are divided by USAspending minor object class. Major classes are retained only when already below $10B; larger classes are split just far enough by minor class and program activity. All eight schedules reconcile to Treasury cents and every leaf remains below $10B.
- Four additional RDT&E availability rows now reconcile from USAspending object-class schedules: Air Force 2023–2024, Defense-Wide 2023–2024 and 2024–2025, and Navy 2024–2025. Marine Corps Military Personnel 2024 also resolves into 16 object classes plus its explicit basis bridge. Air Force Military Personnel 2024 resolves after its $11.062B enlisted-pay object class is nested into the Air Force's published FY2024 actual basic-pay-by-grade schedule and a signed $230.192M basis bridge. Navy Military Personnel 2024 now follows a three-level chain from USAspending's $23.157B enlisted-personnel row through the Navy M-1 activity schedule and then its published basic-pay-by-grade schedule. Army Military Personnel 2024 resolves both of its large USAspending leaves through the Army's published enlisted pay-by-grade and benefit-component schedules. NNSA Weapons Activities no-year resources resolve by nesting its $12.827B direct-facilities leaf into the agency's ten published congressional controls and an explicit signed basis bridge. TANF's 2024 State Family Assistance Grant leaf now reuses ACF's 51-state federal-expenditure schedule through a visible signed reporting-basis bridge. VA Compensation and Pensions no-year resources now resolve through USAspending program activities, with Veterans and Survivors nested into VA's FY2024 address-linked GDX schedules and explicit reporting-basis bridges. Every terminal source and bridge row in these chains is below $10B. Defense Health, Air Force Other Procurement, and Air Force 2024–2025 RDT&E were not promoted because their deepest available program-activity/object-class rows still contain $12.827B–$24.761B leaves.

After the completed federal pass, the account-root audit finds **85** Treasury accounts at or above $10 billion, **85** exact nested source schedules, and **zero** uncovered account roots. Larger rows that remain inside a published lower schedule are explicitly labeled public or classified disclosure ceilings when no truthful deeper additive schedule is available.

### Count reconciliation and retained-row audit

The earlier **446** count excluded **85** large Treasury account cross-check rows because the federal UI labels that catalog “reference only, not a subtotal.” Its underlying audit snapshot contained 531 large rendered occurrences: `531 - 85 = 446`. The candidate snapshot contains 530 because a $62.156 billion Education gross-consolidation-flow context row was removed while this research was in progress. On the same reference-row exclusion, that count is therefore 445: `530 - 85 = 445`. Equivalently, `530 - 85 + 1 removed context row = 446`. The 530 inventory below deliberately retains all visible occurrences; the 85 cross-checks are separately identifiable by `kind = account cross-check`.

The exact baseline queue is [account-reimport-candidates.tsv](account-reimport-candidates.tsv): **74** large-row occurrences and **148** single-child occurrences whose active metadata records more than one underlying row. A recursive exact-array scan of every `data/` tree in all seven repository commits found no retained array with the same jurisdiction, row count, and cent-exact sum for any candidate. During this audit, concurrent uncommitted work added exact Combined Statement availability arrays for 34 of the large federal cross-check candidates; the TSV marks those `current_exact_rows`. The remaining 188 entries are `sourceRows_count_only` and still require row-level re-import from the cited source. For federal account cross-checks, the count is periods of availability rather than transaction records, as recorded in the TSV's `count_semantics` column.

## Audit definition

- Scope: every nonzero row the current UI can render from the 51 jurisdiction summaries, official state archive departments, both signed reconciliation bridges, federal detail panels, Treasury account cross-checks, and the receipt hierarchy.
- Large-row rule: signed absolute amount is **strictly greater than $10,000,000,000**. Repeated appearances are retained because the request applies everywhere in the rendered tree.
- Exact breakdown rule: at least two nonzero official child rows must sum to the parent in integer cents on the same source basis. Contextual, different-period, gross-versus-net, budget-versus-GAAP, and merely labeled ceiling panels do not qualify.
- Single-child rule: a rendered detail category or panel has exactly one nonzero visible child and no exact multi-child panel replacing it.
- “Additive breakdown: No” means no qualifying child set is present and verified in the active evidence. An underlying-row count identifies the best re-import candidate, but is not itself a fabricated allocation or a completed breakdown.

## Primary-source findings and guardrails

The Census Bureau states that its FY2024 product merges like financial items into uniform categories and classifies expenditures by function and object; it is a statistical product, not a state accounting statement. Therefore, a state checkbook or ACFR schedule cannot be attached beneath a Census leaf unless an official same-boundary bridge proves the amounts. [Census FY2024 technical documentation](https://www2.census.gov/programs-surveys/state/technical-documentation/complete-technical-documentation/statetechdoc2024.pdf) · [Census methodology](https://www.census.gov/programs-surveys/state/technical-documentation/methodology.html)

For federal cash outlays, MTS Table 5 is the program-classification control, while the Combined Statement publishes appropriation/fund accounts and periods of availability. Those are valid deeper sources only when their exact signed account rows reconcile to the MTS parent; a large Combined Statement account is still terminal until a lower official row set is verified. [September 2024 MTS](https://fiscal.treasury.gov/system/files/files/reports-statements/mts/mts0924.pdf) · [FY2024 Combined Statement](https://fiscal.treasury.gov/files/reports-statements/combined-statement/cs2024/2024-cs-final.pdf)

The strongest unexposed state candidates are rows whose active metadata preserves more than one official source record: Michigan payment data can be grouped by fiscal year, department, fund, unit, accounting period, object category, object, and check date; New Jersey's expenditure file exposes agency, program, organization, funding, expenditure category, and payee dimensions; Virginia Data Point states that it includes transaction data subject to statutory redactions. These sources can support exact grouped children after the retained FY2024 extracts are re-imported and reconciled—not by prorating the displayed total. [Michigan Payment Search](https://sigmai.michigan.gov/EI360TransparencyApp/jsp/searchPayments) · [New Jersey Agency Expenditures](https://data.nj.gov/w/apet-rp2i/default) · [Virginia Data Point methodology](https://www.datapoint.apa.virginia.gov/about.php)

CMS confirms that FY2024 CMS-64 files contain state/program expenditure summaries and that states can make later prior-period adjustments. Those official service rows are useful source-native context, but they do not automatically reconcile to Treasury cash timing. [CMS FY2024 expenditure reports](https://www.medicaid.gov/medicaid/financial-management/state-budget-expenditure-reporting-for-medicaid-and-chip/expenditure-reports-mbes/cbes)

## Baseline remediation order retained for the evidence trail

1. Re-import and verify exact child values for the 74 large rendered occurrences and 148 single-child occurrences whose metadata records multiple official source rows.
2. For one-row Census, MTS, GAAP, ACFR, and checkbook leaves, locate a deeper official same-period, same-boundary, same-measure schedule. If none exists, the UI cannot honestly satisfy the requested invariant without omitting or regrouping that leaf.
3. Keep privacy-redacted recipients grouped; use non-identifying official dimensions such as program, service, fund, object, geography, or accounting period only when the grouped children reproduce the parent exactly.

## Verification

The baseline inventory was generated from the repository data through the same summary/detail/reconciliation model used by the browser. Zero rows were excluded because the loader suppresses them. Its counts were independently checked against the generated list markers: 530 <code>large-leaf</code> entries and 1291 <code>single-child</code> entries. Current automated checks separately enforce 85/85 federal large-account coverage, reject one-row breakdowns, and require every browser-visible state row at or above $5 billion to be either exactly expanded or explicitly labeled as a source ceiling.
